5. Model Lab browser
5.1 Compatibility score — src/core/compatibility.ts
Pure function. No I/O. Fully testable.

ts
import type { HardwareProfile, CatalogModel } from "../shared/types.js";

export interface CompatibilityScore {
  score: number;        // 0–100
  predictedTokPerSec: number;
  maxSafeContext: number;
  reason: string;
}

/**
 * Estimate how well a model will run on this hardware. The formula is
 * deliberately simple and slightly conservative. It is not a benchmark;
 * it is a coarse filter so users do not download a 32B model onto an 8GB
 * laptop and file a bug report.
 *
 * Inputs:
 *  - model.tier: 1 (<=1.5B), 2 (3B–8B), 3 (>=14B)
 *  - model.quant: currently Q4_K_M for all catalog entries; if you add
 *    Q8 or Q2, extend the sizeMb estimate below.
 *  - hardware.tier and hardware.gpu
 */
export function scoreCompatibility(
  model: CatalogModel,
  hardware: HardwareProfile,
): CompatibilityScore {
  const approxParams: Record<number, number> = { 1: 1.5, 2: 7, 3: 14 };
  const paramsB = approxParams[model.tier] ?? 7;
  const sizeGb = model.sizeBytes / 1024 ** 3;

  const vramGb = (hardware.gpu?.vramBytes ?? 0) / 1024 ** 3;
  const ramGb = hardware.totalRamBytes / 1024 ** 3;

  // Memory fit: can the model live fully in VRAM? If not, in RAM? If neither,
  // it will spill to disk and tok/s collapses.
  let memScore = 0;
  let reason = "";

  if (vramGb >= sizeGb + 1) {
    memScore = 100;
    reason = "Fits entirely in VRAM";
  } else if (vramGb >= sizeGb * 0.6) {
    memScore = 80;
    reason = "Partial VRAM offload";
  } else if (ramGb >= sizeGb + 2) {
    memScore = 60;
    reason = "CPU-only, fits in RAM";
  } else if (ramGb >= sizeGb) {
    memScore = 30;
    reason = "Tight fit, expect slow generation";
  } else {
    memScore = 5;
    reason = "Does not fit; will thrash";
  }

  // Tier floor: a 14B on an 8GB laptop is never a good idea.
  const tierFloor: Record<number, Record<string, number>> = {
    1: { "1": 100, "2": 40, "3": 0 },
    "2": { "1": 90, "2": 80, "3": 30 },
    "2.5": { "1": 90, "2": 80, "3": 40 },
    3: { "1": 70, "2": 100, "3": 100 },
  };
  const floor = tierFloor[hardware.tier as unknown as number]?.[String(model.tier)] ?? 40;
  const score = Math.min(memScore, floor);

  // Predicted tok/s — very rough, tuned to match community reports for
  // Qwen 2.5 Coder Q4_K_M.
  let predictedTokPerSec: number;
  if (vramGb >= sizeGb + 1) {
    predictedTokPerSec = Math.round(30 / Math.sqrt(paramsB / 7));
  } else if (vramGb >= sizeGb * 0.6) {
    predictedTokPerSec = Math.round(15 / Math.sqrt(paramsB / 7));
  } else if (ramGb >= sizeGb + 2) {
    predictedTokPerSec = Math.round(8 / Math.sqrt(paramsB / 7));
  } else {
    predictedTokPerSec = 2;
  }

  // Safe context: be conservative. 4K is always safe; 8K if there is
  // headroom; 16K only on Tier 3 with generous VRAM.
  let maxSafeContext = 4096;
  if (score >= 80 && ramGb >= sizeGb + 6) maxSafeContext = 8192;
  if (score >= 95 && ramGb >= sizeGb + 12) maxSafeContext = 16384;

  return { score, predictedTokPerSec, maxSafeContext, reason };
}
5.2 src/renderer/workspace/ModelLab.tsx
tsx
import { useEffect, useMemo, useState } from "react";
import { ProGate } from "../components/ProGate";
import type {
  CatalogModel,
  HardwareProfile,
  DownloadProgress,
} from "../../shared/types";
import { scoreCompatibility } from "../../core/compatibility";

interface Row extends CatalogModel {
  downloaded: boolean;
}

export function ModelLab({ hardware }: { hardware: HardwareProfile }): JSX.Element {
  return (
    <ProGate
      feature="modelLab"
      title="Model Lab"
      description="Browse and download additional coding models with compatibility scores for your hardware."
    >
      <ModelLabInner hardware={hardware} />
    </ProGate>
  );
}

function ModelLabInner({ hardware }: { hardware: HardwareProfile }): JSX.Element {
  const [rows, setRows] = useState<Row[]>([]);
  const [progress, setProgress] = useState<Record<string, DownloadProgress>>({});
  const [tierFilter, setTierFilter] = useState<"all" | 1 | 2 | 3>("all");
  const [busy, setBusy] = useState<Record<string, boolean>>({});

  useEffect(() => {
    void window.thunder.catalog.list().then(setRows);
    const off = window.thunder.catalog.onProgress((p) => {
      setProgress((prev) => ({ ...prev, [p.modelId]: p }));
      if (p.phase === "done") {
        setBusy((prev) => ({ ...prev, [p.modelId]: false }));
        void window.thunder.catalog.list().then(setRows);
      }
      if (p.phase === "error") {
        setBusy((prev) => ({ ...prev, [p.modelId]: false }));
      }
    });
    return off;
  }, []);

  const visible = useMemo(
    () => rows.filter((r) => tierFilter === "all" || r.tier === tierFilter),
    [rows, tierFilter],
  );

  async function download(id: string): Promise<void> {
    setBusy((prev) => ({ ...prev, [id]: true }));
    try {
      await window.thunder.catalog.downloadStart(id);
    } catch {
      setBusy((prev) => ({ ...prev, [id]: false }));
    }
  }

  return (
    <div className="flex h-full flex-col">
      <div className="flex items-center gap-2 border-b p-2 text-sm">
        <strong>Model Lab</strong>
        <select
          value={tierFilter}
          onChange={(e) =>
            setTierFilter(
              e.target.value === "all"
                ? "all"
                : (Number(e.target.value) as 1 | 2 | 3),
            )
          }
          className="rounded border px-1"
        >
          <option value="all">All sizes</option>
          <option value="1">Small (≤1.5B)</option>
          <option value="2">Medium (3–8B)</option>
          <option value="3">Large (14B+)</option>
        </select>
      </div>
      <div className="flex-1 overflow-y-auto">
        {visible.map((r) => {
          const c = scoreCompatibility(r, hardware);
          const p = progress[r.id];
          return (
            <div key={r.id} className="border-b p-3 text-sm">
              <div className="flex items-center gap-2">
                <strong>{r.displayName}</strong>
                <span className="rounded bg-gray-100 px-1 text-xs">{r.quant}</span>
                <span className="text-xs text-gray-500">
                  {(r.sizeBytes / 1024 ** 3).toFixed(2)} GB · {r.license}
                </span>
                <span className="ml-auto text-xs">
                  Score {c.score}/100 · ~{c.predictedTokPerSec} tok/s · ctx{" "}
                  {(c.maxSafeContext / 1024).toFixed(0)}K
                </span>
              </div>
              <p className="mt-1 text-xs text-gray-600">{c.reason}</p>
              {p && p.phase !== "done" && p.phase !== "error" ? (
                <div className="mt-2">
                  <div className="h-2 rounded bg-gray-200">
                    <div
                      className="h-2 rounded bg-blue-600"
                      style={{
                        width: `${
                          p.totalBytes > 0
                            ? Math.floor((p.downloadedBytes / p.totalBytes) * 100)
                            : 0
                        }%`,
                      }}
                    />
                  </div>
                  <span className="text-xs text-gray-500">{p.phase}</span>
                </div>
              ) : null}
              {p?.phase === "error" ? (
                <p className="mt-1 text-xs text-red-600">{p.error}</p>
              ) : null}
              <div className="mt-2">
                {r.downloaded ? (
                  <span className="text-xs text-green-700">Downloaded</span>
                ) : busy[r.id] ? (
                  <button
                    className="rounded bg-gray-300 px-2 py-0.5 text-xs"
                    onClick={() => void window.thunder.catalog.downloadCancel(r.id)}
                  >
                    Cancel
                  </button>
                ) : (
                  <button
                    className="rounded bg-blue-600 px-2 py-0.5 text-xs text-white disabled:opacity-50"
                    disabled={c.score < 20}
                    onClick={() => void download(r.id)}
                    title={c.score < 20 ? "Not recommended for this hardware" : ""}
                  >
                    Download
                  </button>
                )}
              </div>
            </div>
          );
        })}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}
Deliberate behavior: the Download button is disabled when score < 20.
This is the single most important UX decision in Model Lab. A user who
downloads a 14B Q4 model onto an 8GB laptop, waits 30 minutes, and then gets
2 tok/s will uninstall Thunder. Disabling low-score downloads prevents that
ticket. The title tooltip explains why. This is honest and it is not
patronizing — it is a good product decision.

6. Token-meter context budget
6.1 src/core/inference.ts — add a tokenizer export
Append to the file:

ts
/**
 * Tokenize a string with the model's actual tokenizer. Returns the exact
 * token count the model will see, which is what the context budget must use.
 * Falls back to the char/3.5 heuristic only if the model is not yet loaded.
 */
export async function countTokens(
  modelId: string,
  text: string,
): Promise<number> {
  try {
    const model = await loadModel(modelId);
    const tokens = model.tokenize(text);
    return tokens.length;
  } catch {
    return Math.ceil(text.length / 3.5);
  }
}
Verify LlamaModel#tokenize exists in your pinned node-llama-cpp@3.22.3.
It does (it is the same method LlamaChatSession uses internally for
maxTokens accounting), but confirm with:

6.2 src/core/agent.ts — replace buildContext
Replace the M6 buildContext function with:

ts
interface BuiltContext {
  contextString: string;
  redactionCount: number;
  tokensUsed: number;
}

async function buildContext(
  opts: AgentRunOptions,
  history: string[],
): Promise<BuiltContext> {
  const CONTEXT_BUDGET = 6000; // tokens, across the entire system prompt
  const RESERVE_FOR_PROMPT = 800; // user message + system template overhead
  const budget = CONTEXT_BUDGET - RESERVE_FOR_PROMPT;

  let base = "";
  if (opts.openFilePath) {
    try {
      const content = await opts.io.readFile(
        path.join(opts.workspaceRoot, opts.openFilePath),
      );
      const count = await countTokens(opts.modelId, content);
      if (count <= budget) {
        base = `<file path="${opts.openFilePath}">\n${content}\n</file>`;
      } else {
        // Truncate to a line boundary that fits. Never cut mid-line.
        const lines = content.split("\n");
        let lo = 0;
        let hi = lines.length;
        while (lo < hi) {
          const mid = Math.floor((lo + hi + 1) / 2);
          const chunk = lines.slice(0, mid).join("\n");
          const n = await countTokens(opts.modelId, chunk);
          if (n <= budget) lo = mid;
          else hi = mid - 1;
        }
        const chunk = lines.slice(0, lo).join("\n");
        base = `<file path="${opts.openFilePath}" truncated="true">\n${chunk}\n</file>`;
      }
    } catch {
      // File unreadable; continue without it.
    }
  }

  // Scratchpad: keep the newest entries that fit, drop oldest.
  const scratchBudget = Math.max(0, budget - (await countTokens(opts.modelId, base)));
  const keptScratch: string[] = [];
  let scratchUsed = 0;
  for (let i = history.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const n = await countTokens(opts.modelId, history[i]);
    if (scratchUsed + n > scratchBudget) break;
    keptScratch.unshift(history[i]);
    scratchUsed += n;
  }
  const scratch =
    keptScratch.length > 0
      ? `\n<scratchpad>\n${keptScratch.join("\n")}\n</scratchpad>`
      : "";

  const raw = `${base}${scratch}`;
  const redacted = await redactSecrets(raw);
  const tokensUsed = await countTokens(opts.modelId, redacted.text);
  return {
    contextString: redacted.text,
    redactionCount: redacted.count,
    tokensUsed,
  };
}
And in runAgent, log built.tokensUsed into the AgentStep.payload for the
diagnostic benefit:

ts
// where built is created:
const built = await buildContext(opts, scratchpad);

// when constructing a step's payload, add tokensUsed:
payload: { ..., contextTokens: built.tokensUsed },
Import countTokens from ./inference.js at the top of agent.ts.

ts
import { describe, it, expect, vi } from "vitest";

vi.mock("electron", () => ({
  app: { isPackaged: false, getAppPath: () => process.cwd(), getPath: () => "/tmp" },
}));

// Fake a tokenizer that splits on whitespace. This proves the plumbing
// (countTokens is called, budget is enforced) without needing a real GGUF.
vi.mock("../../src/core/inference.js", () => ({
  countTokens: async (_modelId: string, text: string) =>
    text.trim().split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean).length,
  loadModel: async () => ({}),
  startStream: () => ({ streamId: "x", emitter: {} as never, abort: () => {} }),
}));

import { buildContextForTest } from "../../src/core/agent.js";

describe("token budget", () => {
  it("truncates a file that exceeds the budget at a line boundary", async () => {
    const big = Array.from({ length: 10000 }, (_, i) => `word${i}`).join("\n");
    const built = await buildContextForTest(
      {
        modelId: "m",
        openFilePath: "big.ts",
        workspaceRoot: "/tmp",
        taskId: "t",
        userMessage: "hi",
        tier: 2,
        openFileLine: null,
        abortSignal: new AbortController().signal,
        io: {
          readFile: async () => big,
          listDir: async () => [],
          writeFile: async () => {},
        },
        callbacks: {
          onStep: () => {},
          onToken: () => {},
          onDiff: () => {},
          onTerminalRequest: async () => "deny",
          onFallback: () => {},
          onFinish: () => {},
          onError: () => {},
        },
      },
      [],
    );
    expect(built.tokensUsed).toBeLessThanOrEqual(6000);
    expect(built.contextString).toContain('truncated="true"');
    // Every line in the retained chunk must be a whole line from the source.
    const kept = built.contextString.split("\n").slice(1, -2); // strip wrapper
    for (const line of kept) {
      expect(line).toMatch(/^word\d+$/);
    }
  });

  it("includes the whole file when it fits", async () => {
    const small = "const x = 1;\nconst y = 2;";
    const built = await buildContextForTest(
      {
        modelId: "m",
        openFilePath: "small.ts",
        workspaceRoot: "/tmp",
        taskId: "t",
        userMessage: "hi",
        tier: 2,
        openFileLine: null,
        abortSignal: new AbortController().signal,
        io: {
          readFile: async () => small,
          listDir: async () => [],
          writeFile: async () => {},
        },
        callbacks: {
          onStep: () => {},
          onToken: () => {},
          onDiff: () => {},
          onTerminalRequest: async () => "deny",
          onFallback: () => {},
          onFinish: () => {},
          onError: () => {},
        },
      },
      [],
    );
    expect(built.contextString).toContain(small);
    expect(built.contextString).not.toContain('truncated="true"');
  });
});
Export buildContext as buildContextForTest in agent.ts:

ts
export const buildContextForTest = buildContext;
This is a test seam, not a public API. Mark it in a comment.

7. Plan→Agent handoff
7.1 src/core/agent.ts — accept a prior plan
Add to AgentRunOptions:

ts
/**
 * Optional plan text from a Plan-mode response. When set, the user message is
 * framed as "execute this plan" rather than a fresh task, and the original
 * user request is appended so the model retains the goal. Do not omit the
 * original request — 7B models lose the goal otherwise and over-plan.
 */
priorPlan?: string;
In runAgent, build the effective user message:

ts
const effectiveUserMessage = opts.priorPlan
  ? `Execute this plan step by step. Emit search_replace blocks that implement it.

Plan:
${opts.priorPlan}

Original request: ${opts.userMessage}`
  : opts.userMessage;
Use effectiveUserMessage everywhere opts.userMessage was previously passed
to session.prompt.

7.2 src/main/ipc/agent.ipc.ts — accept the plan
Add priorPlan?: string to the handler payload type. Pass it through to
runAgent. No other change.

7.3 Renderer: "Generate diff" button
In ChatPanel.tsx, when a Plan-mode assistant message exists and no Agent
task has been started for it, show a "Generate diff" button under the plan.
Clicking it:

Generates a fresh taskId.

Calls window.thunder.agent.start({ ..., priorPlan: planText, userMessage: originalUserText }).

Switches the right pane to Agent view.

The original user text must be preserved in the ChatMessage for the handoff.
Add a meta: { userInput?: string; planText?: string } field to the message
shape. When the user sends a Plan request, store both:

ts
setMessages((prev) => [
  ...prev,
  { role: "user", content: text },
  { role: "assistant", content: "", streaming: true, meta: { userInput: text } },
]);
When the Plan stream ends, populate planText:

ts
setMessages((prev) => {
  const copy = [...prev];
  const last = copy[copy.length - 1];
  if (last?.role === "assistant" && last.meta) {
    copy[copy.length - 1] = { ...last, meta: { ...last.meta, planText: last.content } };
  }
  return copy;
});
Then render:

tsx
{m.role === "assistant" && m.meta?.planText && m.meta.userInput ? (
  <button
    className="mt-2 rounded bg-blue-600 px-2 py-0.5 text-xs text-white"
    onClick={() =>
      void window.thunder.agent.start({
        taskId: crypto.randomUUID(),
        userMessage: m.meta!.userInput!,
        priorPlan: m.meta!.planText,
        tier: /* current tier from hardware store */ 2,
        workspaceRoot: /* from workspace store */ "",
        modelId,
        openFilePath: null,
        openFileLine: null,
      })
    }
  >
    Generate diff
  </button>
) : null}
This is intentionally a small UI addition. The substantive change is the
priorPlan framing in agent.ts — that is what makes the handoff work.

Manual smoke test:

text
1. Waitlist
   a. Launch without a license. Model Lab tab shows ProGate.
   b. Click "Join the waitlist". Submit test@example.com with plan "pro".
   c. EXPECT: "You're on the list." message.

      IGNORE), no duplicate row.
   e. Check SQLite: SELECT * FROM waitlist; — exactly one row.
2. Model Lab (Pro)
   a. Activate a valid license (from M7's sign-license script).
   b. Open Model Lab. EXPECT: catalog rows with score/tok-s/ctx for each
      model.
   c. On a Tier 2 machine, the 14B model should show score < 20 and a
      disabled Download button. Hover: "Not recommended for this hardware."
   d. Click Download on the 3B model. EXPECT: progress bar, then
      "Downloaded". SQLite model_registry has the row.
   e. Filter by "Large (14B+)". EXPECT: the 14B row shows, still disabled.
3. Token budget
   a. Open a 3,000-line TypeScript file in the editor. Switch to Agent.
   b. Ask: "Rename the function `parseConfig` to `readConfig` everywhere
      it is used in this file."
   c. After the Agent finishes, check the last `agent_steps` row's payload:
        sqlite3 <userData>/thunder.db "SELECT payload_json FROM agent_steps ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1;"
      EXPECT: contextTokens <= 6000. No error about context overflow.
   d. If the model responds with "I can't see the full file", the truncation
      message was not clear. Improve the `<file truncated="true">` marker's
      text in buildContext to say "truncated to fit context; ask to read a
      specific range". Rerun.
4. Plan→Agent handoff
   a. Switch to Plan mode. Ask: "Add input validation to parseConfig in
      src/config.ts."
   b. When the plan renders, a "Generate diff" button appears under it.
   c. Click it. EXPECT: Agent starts, runs read → search_replace → finish.
      The plan text appears in the effective user message in the main-process
      log. The original request appears at the bottom of that message.
   d. Verify with a task the model can't do from the plan alone: "Refactor
      the three functions in utils.ts to use a shared helper." The plan should
      list three functions; the Agent should touch all three. If it only
      touches one, the prior-plan framing is too loose — see the note in
      section 7.1 and tighten the "Emit search_replace blocks that implement
      it" wording.
5. Regression
   a. Ask mode still works.
   b. Agent without priorPlan still works.
   c. Thunder doctor still returns a valid report.
M8 is complete when all 5 manual sections pass and all 5 automated tests are green.

What M8 deliberately does not do
No waitlist server sync. The waitlist is a local SQLite table. When
you are ready to collect emails, add a server endpoint and a background
uploader. That is a 30-line change, not a milestone.

No download queue. Model Lab downloads run one at a time via the
existing downloadModel. If the user clicks two Download buttons
simultaneously, the second downloadStart throws "already in progress" and
the button re-enables. This is acceptable for v1. A queue is post-MVP.

No multi-file diff preview beyond the current tab. DiffReview from
M5 handles multi-file previews. M8 does not change it.

No AST-slice integration into buildContext. M8 uses whole-file
context with a hard token budget. The M5 assembleContext machinery is
used by Plan mode but not by the Agent loop. Merging them is a quality
improvement, not a blocker. Post-MVP.

code signing stubs, clean-machine verification checklist, and the MVP
definition-of-done).

markdown
# THUNDER — Continuation Spec (M9)
# Paste this AFTER M8. This spec is M9 only:
# electron-builder finalization, code signing stubs, extraResources wiring,
# clean-machine verification, and the MVP definition of done.
# Do not begin M9 until the M8 acceptance checklist is green.

## M9 scope

M9 delivers:
1. Final `electron-builder.yml` with `extraResources` for models.json,
   public key, grammars, and schema.sql
2. `.env.example` + build-time environment variable handling
3. Code-signing stubs (macOS notarize, Windows signtool) — wired but optional
4. `scripts/verify-release.ts` — post-build integrity checks
5. `scripts/clean-machine-checklist.md` — manual verification on a fresh VM
6. `RELEASE.md` — one-page release runbook
7. The MVP definition of done

M9 does NOT add auto-update, crash reporting, or telemetry upload. Those are
M10 backlog. Auto-update especially: shipping an auto-updater without a
signing key is worse than shipping without one, because it teaches users to
click through update prompts that will later be exploited.

## Milestone honesty note

Three realities that this milestone must not paper over:

- **Signed builds are not optional for macOS in 2026.** Gatekeeper on macOS
  15+ blocks unsigned `.dmg` files from being opened without the user
  right-clicking → Open → confirming. For a product pitched at "students and
  indie developers," this friction is a churn event. If you cannot afford an
  Apple Developer account ($99/yr) before first release, ship as an unsigned
  `.dmg` with an in-app notice: "macOS will warn that this app is from an
  unidentified developer. Right-click → Open to launch." Honest is fine.
  Silent breakage is not.

- **Windows SmartScreen will flag unsigned `.exe` files.** An EV code-signing
  cert runs $300–$500/yr. If you skip it, users see "Windows protected your
  PC" for the first ~200 downloads. Post a SHA-256 of the release artifact
  alongside the download link so users can verify manually. This does not
  remove the warning, but it does build trust with the audience that checks.

- **Linux is the easy target.** `.AppImage` on Linux requires no signing,
  no notarization, no SmartScreen. For a first release, prioritize Linux as
  the primary distribution. Use macOS and Windows as secondary until you
  have the signing budget.

If you want to skip signing entirely for v1, the honest product decision is:
**ship Linux AppImage first, macOS unsigned with a warning, Windows unsigned
with a warning.** That is defensible. Pretending otherwise is not.

## 1. `electron-builder.yml` (final)

```yaml
appId: dev.thunder.app
productName: Thunder
copyright: Copyright © 2026 Thunder
directories:
  output: release
  buildResources: resources
files:
  - dist/**/*
  - package.json
  - node_modules/**/*
  - "!node_modules/.cache/**/*"
  - "!node_modules/**/*.md"
  - "!node_modules/**/*.map"
asar: true
asarUnpack:
  # node-llama-cpp ships native binaries that cannot be loaded from an asar.
  - "node_modules/node-llama-cpp/**/*"
  - "node_modules/better-sqlite3/**/*"
extraResources:
  - from: resources/models.json
    to: models.json
  - from: resources/grammars
    to: grammars
  - from: resources/grammars.lock.json
    to: grammars.lock.json
  - from: public/thunder-public-key.pem
    to: thunder-public-key.pem
  - from: dist/main/db/schema.sql
    to: schema.sql
mac:
  target:
    - target: dmg
      arch: [arm64, x64]
  category: public.app-category.developer-tools
  hardenedRuntime: true
  gatekeeperAssess: false
  entitlements: build/entitlements.mac.plist
  entitlementsInherit: build/entitlements.mac.plist
  # Code signing is opt-in via env vars; see section 3.
  identity: null
win:
  target:
    - target: nsis
      arch: [x64]
  # Code signing is opt-in via env vars; see section 3.
  signingHashAlgorithms: [sha256]
linux:
  target:
    - target: AppImage
      arch: [x64]
  category: Development
nsis:
  oneClick: false
  perMachine: false
  allowToChangeInstallationDirectory: true
  createDesktopShortcut: true
  createStartMenuShortcut: true
publish: null
asarUnpack note: node-llama-cpp loads native .node bindings and
.gguf shim files from disk. Without unpacking, llama.loadModel fails with
Cannot find module at runtime even though the build succeeds. This is the
single most common electron-builder trap for native-module apps. Verify it
by launching the packaged app and running an Ask query — if it fails, check
app.asar.unpacked/node_modules/node-llama-cpp/ exists.

2. build/entitlements.mac.plist
xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<!DOCTYPE plist PUBLIC "-//Apple//DTD PLIST 1.0//EN" "http://www.apple.com/DTDs/PropertyList-1.0.dtd">
<plist version="1.0">
<dict>
  <key>com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit</key>
  <true/>
  <key>com.apple.security.cs.allow-unsigned-executable-memory</key>
  <true/>
  <key>com.apple.security.cs.disable-library-validation</key>
  <true/>
  <key>com.apple.security.cs.allow-dyld-environment-variables</key>
  <true/>
  <key>com.apple.security.network.client</key>
  <true/>
</dict>
</plist>
allow-jit and allow-unsigned-executable-memory are required because
llama.cpp JIT-compiles Metal kernels on Apple Silicon. Without them, the
first inference call crashes with a kern_return_t violation that is
unhelpful to debug. disable-library-validation is required because
node-llama-cpp loads its native binding via dlopen.

3. Code signing — opt-in via environment variables
The build works without signing. If the following environment variables
are set, electron-builder signs automatically:

macOS:

text
CSC_LINK=/path/to/DeveloperIDApplication.p12
CSC_KEY_PASSWORD=<password>
APPLE_ID=<apple-id-email>
APPLE_APP_SPECIFIC_PASSWORD=<app-specific-password>
APPLE_TEAM_ID=<10-char-team-id>
Windows:

text
CSC_LINK=/path/to/cert.pfx
CSC_KEY_PASSWORD=<password>
If these are unset, pnpm package produces unsigned artifacts. The
build logs a one-line warning at start:

text
[build] No signing credentials found. Producing unsigned artifacts.
[build] macOS: users must right-click → Open to launch.
[build] Windows: SmartScreen will warn for the first ~200 downloads.
This warning is not decoration. It must appear so that whoever builds the
release knows what they are shipping.

4. scripts/verify-release.ts
Run after pnpm package. Catches the failure modes that clean-machine
testing finds ten minutes too late.

ts
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import crypto from "node:crypto";
import { execSync } from "node:child_process";

interface Check {
  name: string;
  ok: boolean;
  detail: string;
}

const checks: Check[] = [];

function check(name: string, fn: () => string): void {
  try {
    checks.push({ name, ok: true, detail: fn() });
  } catch (err) {
    checks.push({
      name,
      ok: false,
      detail: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err),
    });
  }
}

const releaseDir = path.join(process.cwd(), "release");

check("release directory exists", () => {
  if (!fs.existsSync(releaseDir)) throw new Error("release/ not found");
  return releaseDir;
});

check("dist/main built", () => {
  const f = path.join(process.cwd(), "dist", "main", "index.js");
  if (!fs.existsSync(f)) throw new Error("dist/main/index.js missing");
  return "ok";
});

check("dist/renderer built", () => {
  const f = path.join(process.cwd(), "dist", "renderer", "index.html");
  if (!fs.existsSync(f)) throw new Error("dist/renderer/index.html missing");
  return "ok";
});

check("schema.sql in dist/main/db", () => {
  const f = path.join(process.cwd(), "dist", "main", "db", "schema.sql");
  if (!fs.existsSync(f)) throw new Error("schema.sql not copied to dist");
  return "ok";
});

check("grammars.lock.json present", () => {
  const f = path.join(process.cwd(), "resources", "grammars.lock.json");
  if (!fs.existsSync(f)) throw new Error("grammars.lock.json missing");
  const lock = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(f, "utf-8")) as Record<string, string>;
  const expected = Object.keys(lock).length;
  const found = fs
    .readdirSync(path.join(process.cwd(), "resources", "grammars"))
    .filter((n) => n.endsWith(".wasm")).length;
  if (found < expected) {
    throw new Error(`expected ${expected} grammars, found ${found}`);
  }
  return `${found} grammar(s)`;
});

check("public key present", () => {
  const f = path.join(process.cwd(), "public", "thunder-public-key.pem");
  if (!fs.existsSync(f)) throw new Error("public key missing");
  const pem = fs.readFileSync(f, "utf-8");
  if (!pem.includes("BEGIN PUBLIC KEY")) {
    throw new Error("public key is not a PEM SPKI block");
  }
  return "ok";
});

check("models.json parses and has real hashes", () => {
  const f = path.join(process.cwd(), "resources", "models.json");
  if (!fs.existsSync(f)) throw new Error("models.json missing");
  const cat = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(f, "utf-8")) as {
    models: Array<{ id: string; sha256: string }>;
  };
  if (!Array.isArray(cat.models) || cat.models.length === 0) {
    throw new Error("models.json has no models");
  }
  for (const m of cat.models) {
    if (!/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/i.test(m.sha256)) {
      throw new Error(`model ${m.id}: sha256 is not a 64-char hex string`);
    }
  }
  return `${cat.models.length} model(s)`;
});

check("no private key committed", () => {
  const forbidden = [
    "scripts/thunder-private-key.pem",
    "thunder-private-key.pem",
  ];
  for (const f of forbidden) {
    if (fs.existsSync(path.join(process.cwd(), f))) {
      throw new Error(`PRIVATE KEY FOUND AT ${f} — remove before release`);
    }
  }
  // Also check git status if available.
  try {
    const out = execSync("git ls-files", { encoding: "utf-8" });
    if (out.includes("thunder-private-key.pem")) {
      throw new Error("PRIVATE KEY IS TRACKED IN GIT — remove immediately");
    }
  } catch {
    // Not a git repo; skip.
  }
  return "ok";
});

check("no .env committed", () => {
  try {
    const out = execSync("git ls-files", { encoding: "utf-8" });
    const hits = out
      .split("\n")
      .filter((l) => /^\.env(\.|$)/.test(l) && l !== ".env.example");
    if (hits.length > 0) {
      throw new Error(`.env files tracked: ${hits.join(", ")}`);
    }
  } catch {
    // Not a git repo; skip.
  }
  return "ok";
});

// Artifact hashes
check("artifacts present", () => {
  const files = fs
    .readdirSync(releaseDir, { withFileTypes: true })
    .filter((e) => e.isFile())
    .map((e) => e.name)
    .filter((n) => /\.(dmg|exe|AppImage)$/i.test(n));
  if (files.length === 0) throw new Error("no installer artifacts in release/");
  const lines = files.map((f) => {
    const buf = fs.readFileSync(path.join(releaseDir, f));
    const h = crypto.createHash("sha256").update(buf).digest("hex");
    return `${f}  ${h}`;
  });
  fs.writeFileSync(
    path.join(releaseDir, "SHA256SUMS.txt"),
    lines.join("\n") + "\n",
    "utf-8",
  );
  return files.join(", ");
});

// Print results
let failed = 0;
for (const c of checks) {
  const mark = c.ok ? "✓" : "✗";
  console.log(`${mark} ${c.name}: ${c.detail}`);
  if (!c.ok) failed += 1;
}

if (failed > 0) {
  console.error(`\n${failed} check(s) failed. Do NOT release.`);
  process.exit(1);
}

console.log("\nAll release checks passed.");
console.log(`SHA-256 sums written to ${path.join(releaseDir, "SHA256SUMS.txt")}`);
Add to package.json scripts:

json
"verify:release": "tsx scripts/verify-release.ts"
5. scripts/clean-machine-checklist.md
The manual checklist that verify-release.ts cannot replace. Print this and
check every box before declaring a release ready.

markdown
# Thunder — Clean Machine Verification Checklist

Run on: a fresh VM or spare machine with **no Node, no Python, no prior
Thunder install**. The OS must match a target you intend to support.

## Preconditions
- [ ] Fresh VM or unused user account
- [ ] No `node` on PATH (`which node` returns nothing)
- [ ] No `python3` on PATH (`which python3` returns nothing)
- [ ] No `~/.config/thunder`, `~/Library/Application Support/Thunder`,
      or `%APPDATA%\Thunder`
- [ ] At least 20 GB free disk (models.json hydration needs headroom)
- [ ] Network enabled for the first run (downloads models)
- [ ] Network **disabled** for the Zero Outbound Mode test

## Install
- [ ] Installer runs to completion without admin rights (per-user install)
- [ ] Launch from the Start menu / Applications / AppImage double-click
- [ ] No "unidentified developer" hard block (macOS: right-click → Open
      works; the warning appears but is bypassable)
- [ ] No SmartScreen hard block (Windows: "Run anyway" is available)
- [ ] App window appears within 5 seconds on a cold start

## Onboarding
- [ ] Step 1 shows the 3-line promise
- [ ] Step 2 shows the correct tier for the VM's hardware (write it down;
      verify against `thunder doctor` later)
- [ ] Step 3 shows the download size and target folder
- [ ] Clicking "Start download" begins downloading visible progress
- [ ] Kill the app mid-download, relaunch, and verify Step 3 resumes from
      the partial download (not from zero)
- [ ] Full download completes; Step 4 shows "done"

## Ask / Plan / Agent
- [ ] Ask mode responds within 10 seconds (cold) / 2 seconds (warm)
- [ ] Plan mode produces a numbered plan with file references
- [ ] Agent mode on this hardware:
      - If tier 1: the Agent option is visible but disabled with a reason
      - If tier 2/2.5: run a 2-file task. It should complete within the
        tier budget or fall back to sequential cards
      - If tier 3: run a 3-file task. It should complete
- [ ] Cancel button on streaming stops within 500ms (measure with a stopwatch)

## Security
- [ ] Opening a file with `.env` in the name fails with "Read denied"
- [ ] Asking the Agent to edit `.vscode/settings.json` triggers the confirm
      dialog
- [ ] Asking the Agent to edit a normal file proceeds with a diff preview
- [ ] "Accept all" is disabled when the staged diff has a syntax error
- [ ] Terminal approval dialog shows the exact command and does not execute
      it (verify with `ps` or by checking for a side effect)

## Zero Outbound Mode
- [ ] Toggle Zero Outbound Mode on (Settings → Privacy)
- [ ] Open Wireshark (Linux/macOS) or `nettop` (macOS) or Resource Monitor
      → Network (Windows). Filter to the Thunder process only.
- [ ] Ask a question. Verify **zero outbound network requests** during the
      response.
- [ ] Kill the network entirely (`sudo ifconfig en0 down` or VM network
      adapter off). Ask a question. Verify the app works offline.
- [ ] Restore network.

## Pro entitlements
- [ ] Without a license: Model Lab shows the ProGate with the $49/year CTA
- [ ] Paste a valid license. Model Lab unlocks.
- [ ] Restart. Entitlement persists.
- [ ] Paste a tampered license. Activation fails with "signature invalid"
- [ ] Paste an expired license. Activation fails with "expired"

## Thunder doctor
- [ ] Open the doctor modal. "Copy Report" places valid JSON in the clipboard
- [ ] "Save .json" writes a file with the same content
- [ ] The report does NOT contain: file contents, prompts, completions,
      the user's email, or the license JSON. Confirm by grepping.

## Quit / relaunch
- [ ] Close the app cleanly. No crash dialog.
- [ ] Relaunch. Model cache is warm (Ask responds within 3 seconds).
- [ ] Uninstall via OS. Confirm no residual files in the app data directory
      beyond logs the user would expect to keep.

## Uninstall
- [ ] Uninstaller removes the app binary
- [ ] User data directory (`~/Library/Application Support/Thunder`,
      `%APPDATA%\Thunder`, `~/.config/thunder`) is **preserved** by default
      (users expect their downloaded models to survive a reinstall)
- [ ] Provide a "Delete all user data" option in Settings for users who
      want a clean slate

## Sign-off
- OS tested: ______________________
- Version tested: ______________________
- Tester: ______________________
- Date: ______________________
- Any failed checks (must be 0): _______
6. RELEASE.md
markdown
# Thunder Release Runbook

## Build

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm build:main
pnpm build:renderer
pnpm test
pnpm exec tsc -p tsconfig.node.json --noEmit
Package (unsigned — for internal testing)
bash
pnpm package
pnpm verify:release
Package (signed — for public release)
Set the signing env vars first (see electron-builder.yml comments), then:

bash
CSC_LINK=... CSC_KEY_PASSWORD=... APPLE_ID=... APPLE_APP_SPECIFIC_PASSWORD=... APPLE_TEAM_ID=... \
  pnpm package
pnpm verify:release
Verify
pnpm verify:release must print "All release checks passed."

Run scripts/clean-machine-checklist.md on a fresh VM for each target OS.

Compute SHA-256 of each artifact in release/. The SHA256SUMS.txt file
is generated by verify:release.

Post the SHA-256 next to the download link on the release page.

Distribute
Linux: AppImage in release/

macOS: .dmg in release/ (sign, notarize)

Windows: .exe in release/ (sign if cert available)

Post-release
Watch for crash reports (none in v1 — user must email)

Watch the waitlist email volume

Do NOT auto-update. v1 has no updater. Users install new releases manually.

Rollback
If a release is broken:

Delete the GitHub Release / download link.

Post a notice with the previous SHA-256.

Fix, rebuild, republish with the next patch version.

text

## 7. `package.json` scripts (final)

```json
"scripts": {
  "dev": "vite",
  "build:main": "tsc -p tsconfig.node.json && cpx src/main/db/schema.sql dist/main/db/",
  "build:renderer": "vite build",
  "build": "pnpm build:main && pnpm build:renderer",
  "package": "pnpm build && electron-builder",
  "verify:release": "tsx scripts/verify-release.ts",
  "test": "vitest run",
  "test:e2e": "playwright test",
  "lint": "eslint . --ext .ts,.tsx",
  "keypair": "tsx scripts/generate-keypair.ts",
  "sign-license": "tsx scripts/sign-license.ts",
  "fetch-grammars": "tsx scripts/fetch-grammars.ts",
  "verify:hashes": "tsx scripts/verify-hashes.ts"
}
8. MVP definition of done
Thunder v1 is "done" when every line below is true:

Functional
□ Free tier runs on tier 1, 2, 2.5, and 3 hardware with correct
capability gating
□ Ask, Plan, and Agent modes work on tier 2+ hardware
□ Agent mode is honestly disabled on tier 1 with a visible reason
□ Diff-first edits with syntax validation and one bounded retry
□ Terminal approval dialog appears and does not execute (v1 limitation,
documented in the dialog)
□ Zero Outbound Mode blocks all outbound requests, verified with a
network monitor
□ thunder doctor produces a valid DiagnosticReport
□ Pro tier gated by Ed25519 license; offline grace works
□ Waitlist form persists to SQLite; no server sync
□ Model Lab browser lists catalog with compatibility scores and 1-click
download
Security
□ classifyPath denies reads of ~/.ssh, ~/.aws, .env, *.pem,
*.key, id_rsa*, credentials, secrets/
□ classifyPath requires confirmation for writes to .vscode/,
.git/hooks/, .github/workflows/, .thunder/, shell dotfiles
□ redactSecrets strips AWS/GitHub/OpenAI/Anthropic/Stripe/Slack
tokens, JWTs, and PEM blocks before context assembly
□ Terminal commands never auto-approve
□ requiresTerminalApproval returns true unconditionally
Quality
□ Every milestone's automated tests pass
□ Every milestone's manual checklist is signed off
□ No any in production code
□ ESLint and tsc --noEmit clean
□ Clean-machine checklist passed on at least Linux and one of
macOS/Windows
Release hygiene
□ pnpm verify:release passes
□ SHA-256 of each artifact published
□ scripts/thunder-private-key.pem is not in git and not in any
build artifact
□ public/thunder-public-key.pem matches the private key in use
□ No .env files committed
□ models.json hashes verified locally and updated if needed
Out of scope for v1 (post-MVP)
Real payment checkout (MoR integration)

Team tier policy files

Terminal command execution sandbox

Auto-update

Crash reporting / telemetry upload

Model Lab remote catalog merge

BYOK cloud fallback

Multi-window workspace

AST-slice integration into Agent context

Download queue

9. M9 acceptance checklist
bash
# 1. Ensure schema.sql is copied by the build.
pnpm build:main
ls dist/main/db/schema.sql
# EXPECT: exists

# 2. Package unsigned for internal test.
pnpm package

# 3. Verify artifacts.
pnpm verify:release
# EXPECT: "All release checks passed."
# EXPECT: release/SHA256SUMS.txt exists

# 4. Signing (only if env vars are set):
echo $CSC_LINK
# If empty, the unsigned build is the deliverable for now.
Manual verification:

text
1. Run scripts/clean-machine-checklist.md on:
   a. A fresh Ubuntu 24.04 VM (AppImage)
   b. A fresh macOS VM or unused account (dmg)
   c. A fresh Windows 11 VM (nsis)

2. For each OS, every checkbox in the checklist must pass.
   Any failure blocks release.

3. Copy the artifacts and SHA256SUMS.txt into the release staging directory.

4. Post a release notes file with:
   - What works (Free + Pro gate, Agent on tier 2+)
   - What does not work (terminal execution, checkout, auto-update)
   - How to activate Pro manually (sign-license.ts + paste license)
   - SHA-256 of each artifact

5. Do NOT announce the release until all three OS checklists pass.
M9 is complete when pnpm verify:release passes on all three OS targets and scripts/clean-machine-checklist.md is signed off for each.

What M9 deliberately does not do
No auto-updater. publish: null in electron-builder.yml is
intentional. Adding an updater requires a signing key and a server, and an
unsigned updater is a security vulnerability that gets exploited. Ship
manual downloads in v1. Add the updater in M10 with signing in place.

No crash reporting. If a user hits a crash, they copy the thunder doctor report and email it. This is how developer tools worked for 20
years and it is adequate for the first 1,000 users.

No telemetry upload. Local SQLite only, per the original spec's hard
constraint.

No notarization if you skip signing. Notarization is Apple's
server-side scan. It requires a paid Developer account. If you skip it,
ship unsigned and be honest in the release notes.

No Windows ARM64 build. The catalog was tuned for x64 and arm64 (Apple
Silicon). Windows ARM64 is a real platform in 2026 but adds a fourth build
target and a fourth clean-machine checklist. Post-MVP.

The MVP ships when
You can hand an installer to 20 developers, they install it, onboard, run an
Ask query, run an Agent task on a project they care about, and no one files
a "the app won't start" bug. That is the bar. Not "it has every feature."
Not "the MoR works." "It starts, it responds, and it does not break their
machine."

Reply "M10" if you want the post-MVP backlog spec (MoR integration, terminal
sandbox, auto-update, team tier). Otherwise, run M1 through M9 and ship.
